Then the Tathāgata Amoghasiddhi, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, generated the family of all the tathāgatas, and in order to gather all the accomplishments of this extensive rite of the great ritual king of all the tathāgatas, he spoke this heart mantra of the mudrā of all the tathāgatas: oṃ vajra karma muṣṭi haṃ

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, through his own blessing, generated the family of all the tathāgatas of the Blessed One Vairocana, and in order to gather all the accomplishments of this extensive rite of the great ritual king of the family of all the tathāgatas, he taught this maṇḍala of the four mudrās, called Vajrasiddhi.

Next, what is to be fully explained
Is the sublime mudrā maṇḍala.
Because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
It is also known as Vajrasiddhi.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
The wise should fully bestow it.
First set down the image of the Buddha,
And then draw the four mudrās as well.
In the center of the moon disks,
Draw the vajra mudrā and the others.

Then, having performed in full the rites of summoning and the rest in this mudrā maṇḍala of Vajrasiddhi, one should likewise lead the disciple in and say this: You must not show this secret chapter to anyone at all. Why is that? Because there are also beings, people who hold wrong views and commit evil deeds.

There are also beings of little diligence, without application, and who do not know how to draw, and because of their little diligence they do not remain in the great maṇḍalas of the families of all the tathāgatas, such as the Vajradhātu. For their sake, this mudrā maṇḍala of Vajrasiddhi, which has become the samaya of the maṇḍalas of the families of all the tathāgatas, has been blessed to protect completely the realms of beings without exception, to bring them to experience every benefit, happiness, and mental joy, up to the supreme vajra accomplishment of all the tathāgatas. Do not lack trust and faith in these secret samaya mudrās of the families of all the tathāgatas, lest you fall into the realms of hell beings, animals, or hungry ghosts, or die an untimely death without having abandoned unhappiness.

Having charged the disciple thus, one removes the covering from the face and shows the maṇḍala.

Then one recites this samaya of the mudrās of all the tathāgatas:

Whatever great being it may be,
Whatever mudrā is bound,
For the sake of the heart mantra of recitation,
One should meditate on oneself as that one.
Through the way of this wisdom,
Just as Vajrapāṇi has taught,
All the mudrās of the great beings
Will be accomplished by all of them.

Then the secret of all the mudrās should be declared:

Having released one's own faculty,
One should hold it with the vajra binding.
Whatever mudrā one touches with it,
Immediately comes under one's power.

Then the nature of reality of all the mudrās should be declared:

Joining them in the manner of the subtle vajra,
One should bind the wisdom mudrā.
Through the way of this rite,
The wisdom mudrā comes under one's power.

Then the activity of all the mudrās should be declared:

Song and dance, food and dance,
The pleasures of enjoyment and the rest:
If one presents them to the buddhas,
The karma mudrā comes under one's power.

Then one should train in the wisdom of the secret mudrās of accomplishment in all the maṇḍalas:

Having oneself made the sign stable,
One dwells upon a cloth that has become white.
Blessing the sign as a stūpa,
One meditates on oneself as the Vajradhātu.
Making the vajra binding firm,
One sets the middle fingers side by side like a sprout,
And raises the little fingers and the tips of the index fingers:
This is the samaya of the supreme samaya.
In the manner of the subtle vajra,
One meditates in perfect equipoise.
This is the maṇḍala of the subtle vajra,
The way by which samādhi comes under one's power.
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
One holds the two firmly as a vajra;
Crossing the index fingers and the little fingers
Is the maṇḍala of supreme vajra accomplishment.

Then, having trained in the four great mudrā bindings of Sattvavajra and the rest, one should perform the extensive rite of the great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu just as it is. Just as with the four-mudrā maṇḍala of Vajrasiddhi, if one draws all the maṇḍalas, such as the maṇḍala of Akṣobhya, in the manner of a four-mudrā maṇḍala, each with its own mudrās, all accomplishments will be achieved. Likewise, if one draws them on cloth, on walls, in the sky, or in any place at all, all accomplishments will be achieved.

Counting from the examination of the maṇḍala, some succeed as they wish at that very time in the maṇḍala; some, beginning from then, within one day and night; some within four days; some within sixteen days; and some, even those who have committed the five acts of immediate retribution, accomplishing every aim with ease, just as they wish, and experiencing the bliss of every joy, dance, food, and enjoyment, attain the supreme within one year. Thus spoke the Blessed One.

Then again the Blessed One Vairocana entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the samaya vajra of the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas, and this heart mantra of all the tathāgatas, called the Great Vehicle Realization of All the Tathāgatas, came forth from his own heart: vajra satva

Then the Blessed One, the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, taught this maṇḍala of the full perfection of the Great Vehicle, in order to protect completely the realms of all beings, without exception and with none left out, to bring them to experience every benefit, happiness, and mental joy, up to bringing about the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas.

Next, what is to be fully explained
Is the sublime maṇḍala of the sattva.
Because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
It is also called Vajrasattva.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
One should lay down the lines of the outer tier of the maṇḍala.
In the center of the moon disk,
One should place Vajrasattva.

Then, having performed the summoning, the bringing in, and the rest just as they should be done, one should generate the wisdom that attains the accomplishment of all the tathāgatas. One should then charge the disciple, saying: Do not speak of this secret to anyone at all who has not seen the samaya and has not understood it, lest you fall into all the lower realms, or die an untimely death through not having abandoned unhappiness.

Then one should train in the wisdom that accomplishes the supreme accomplishment of Vajrasattva:

Seated upon the full moon,
Having taken up the great mudrā,
If one meditates on oneself as Vajrasattva,
One will swiftly attain accomplishment.

Then one should train in the wisdom of the secret samaya of all the maṇḍalas:

There is no other evil in the three realms
Like being parted from passion.
Therefore you, too,
Must never be parted from desire's passion.

mahā samaya hana phaṭ

Then one gives this heart mantra of the oath. Then, in the extensive rites of the maṇḍalas of all the tathāgatas, one should bestow the vow of samaya. Then one should train in binding the four, beginning with the great mudrā of Vajrasattva. The accomplishments are likewise. Having performed the rite as one wishes on drawings and the like, and on all cast images as well, all accomplishments will likewise be achieved. It should also be done just as the extensive rite of the great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu is performed.

Then again all the tathāgatas gathered together as one and praised the Blessed One, lord of all the tathāgatas, the great awakening mind, Vajrasattva, the great Vajrapāṇi, with this heart mantra of vajra delight:

oṃ Vajrasattva, you are excellent!
Excellent are you, Jewel Vajra!
You are Vajradharma;
Vajrakarma, you are excellent!
The unsurpassed vajra vehicle,
The secret of all the tathāgatas,
This sūtra, the compendium of the manifest Great Vehicle,
Has been well taught.
The characteristics of Vajrasattva, too,
Become the supreme of all accomplishments.
If one practices having been purified,
One attains buddhahood itself with ease.
The union of Vajradharma
Is blissful with the bliss of all desires;
Because it is accomplished in this very life,
Its bliss is inexhaustible and will not cease.

From the Compendium of the Reality of All the Tathāgatas, the first section of the great ritual king called the Great Vehicle Realization of All the Tathāgatas is complete.

The fourth fascicle.

Then again the blessed tathāgatas all gathered together as one and petitioned the Blessed One, lord of all the tathāgatas, who turns the great wheel, with these hundred and eight names:

Vajrasattva, great vajra,
Vajra protector, you who bring perfect accomplishment,
Vajra empowerment, vajra light,
Vajra glory, homage to you!
Vajra laughter, great Dharma,
Vajra sword, great and supreme,
Supreme king of all the maṇḍalas,
Free of elaboration, homage to you!
Vajra action and great protection,
Fierce yakṣa, great graha,
Vajramuṣṭi, great mudrā,
Homage to all the mudrās!
Awakening mind and great awakening,
All the buddhas, the tathāgatas,
Vajra wisdom, great wisdom,
Great Vehicle, homage to you!
The very reality and meaning of all aims,
Knower of all the great aims of beings,
All-knowing, all-doing, the all,
All-seeing one, homage to you!
Supreme vajra self, supreme vajra,
Vajra of supreme diligence, Vajradhara,
Great samaya, the meaning of reality,
Great mind, homage to you!
Vajrāṅkuśa, great desire,
Utter delight, great light,
Vajra light and clear luminosity,
Light of the buddhas, homage to you!
Supreme vajra king, fierce vajra,
Supreme of the supreme family, supreme among humans,
Sublime vajra, supreme of the great supreme,
Supreme family, homage to you!
Vajradhātu and great secret,
Vajraguhya, holder of the highest secret,
Subtle vajra, great meditative concentration,
Vajra purpose, homage to you!
Supreme buddha, awakened one, supreme vajra,
Buddha awakening, great learned one,
Buddha wisdom, great buddha,
Buddha of buddhas, homage to you!
Buddha offering, great offering,
Sattva offering, supreme offering,
Great means and great accomplishment,
Vajra accomplishment, homage to you!
Great body of the tathāgatas,
Melodious speech of the tathāgatas,
Great mind of the tathāgatas,
Vajra of vajras, homage to you!
Chief of buddhas, who hears the Victors' command,
Chief of buddhas, born as the spiritual friend,
Vairocana, great and all-pervading,
Teacher and wrathful one, homage to you!
Great reality of the tathāgatas,
The true limit, the way of greatness,
Perfection, all-knowing one,
Ultimate one, homage to you!
Samantabhadra and supreme conduct,
Utter tamer of Māra and the māras,
Knower of all as supreme and as equality,
All-pervading one, homage to you!
Buddha who says hūṃ, who says hūṃ,
Tamer who says vajra hūṃ,
Vajraviśva, supreme vajra,
Vajrapāṇi, homage to you!
Homage, offering, and praise,
And service have been rendered by the tathāgatas.
Because your vajra mind is steadfast,
You are called Vajrasattva.
Perfect awakening depends on you;
You are the father of the All-Seeing One;
The tathāgatas who have come and who are yet to come
Have been accomplished by you.
Whoever praises with this king of praises
With intense devotion,
Whoever praises it by singing it as a song,
Will become like Vajradhara.
O protector who holds sway over all the buddhas,
We beseech you:
For the sake of accomplishing the aims of all beings,
Please generate your own family.

Then Vajrapāṇi, lord of all the tathāgatas, having heard the words of petition of all the tathāgatas, placed the vajra at his own heart, called all those tathāgatas, and spoke thus: "Blessed tathāgatas, I cannot."

All the tathāgatas said, "Why not?"

Vajradhara said, "Blessed Ones, there are wicked beings such as Maheśvara (Śiva), whom even all the tathāgatas do not set about taming by peaceful means. How am I to accomplish this with them?"

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, through the blessing of all the tathāgatas, entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the vajra of the wisdom of the great means of all the tathāgatas. As soon as he had entered into equipoise, all the tathāgatas streamed out, equal in number to the most subtle particles of the whole expanse of space, and gathered together once more in the peaked pavilion made of vajra, gems, and jewels on the summit of Mount Meru, its vajra peak. Focusing on equality, all the tathāgatas entered the śrīvatsa at the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana.

Then again the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Vairocana, blessed himself as the mind of all the tathāgatas. In order, through the vajra equality of all the tathāgatas, to protect completely the realms of beings without exception and with none left out, to accomplish every benefit and supreme happiness, and to tame all the wicked, he focused on the wisdom of the meditative absorption of the means of the great compassion of all the tathāgatas, and entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the samaya vajra of the great wrathful one, the means of the great compassion of all the tathāgatas. As soon as he had entered into equipoise, this heart mantra of all the tathāgatas, called the vajra samaya of all the tathāgatas, came forth from the heart of all the tathāgatas: hūṃ

Then, as soon as it had come forth, from the vajra at the heart of Vajrapāṇi there came forth the form of Vajrapāṇi, the Blessed One Vajradhara himself: the essence of all-blazing fire, wrathfully scowling, his brows knitted and his forehead furrowed, his fangs bared and his teeth clenched, holding in his hands weapons blazing with vajra flames, such as the vajra, the hook, the sword, and the noose, and wearing the garb of many colors and ornaments. Having tamed all the utterly wicked in all the world systems, he took his seat on a moon disk on every side of the Blessed One Vairocana, in the manner of the great maṇḍala of the Vajradhātu, and uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! I am the one
Endowed with the great means that tames through means:
Because it is a means of taming beings,
I have become a stainless wrathful one.

Then the Tathāgata Vairocana, focusing on the nature of reality, free of elaboration, of all the tathāgatas, entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the vajra blessing of the vajra samaya of the great wrathful one of all the tathāgatas, and spoke this supreme knowledge mantra, the heart of all the tathāgatas, called the vajra hūṃ of all the tathāgatas: oṃ sumbha ni sumbha hūṃ gṛ hṇa gṛhṇa hūṃ gṛhṇāpaya gṛhṇāpaya hūṃ ānaya hoḥ bhagavāṃ vajra hūṃ phaṭ

Then, as soon as it had come forth from the heart of all the tathāgatas, it became the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi. Having come forth, in all the clouds of the boundless ocean of world systems he blessed all the tathāgatas together with their maṇḍalas of bodhisattva retinues, invited them, brought them into the great maṇḍala of the vajra samaya, and bound them with the samaya. Then they gathered together as one again, became the body of Vajracaṇḍa, took their place at the heart of the Blessed One Vairocana, and uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! This awakening mind
Is all-good and free of evil;
Because it is for the sake of taming beings,
Even wrath becomes delightful.

Then that body of Vajracaṇḍa descended from the heart of the Blessed One and stayed upon a moon disk before all the tathāgatas, and remained there, again awaiting command.

Then the Blessed One entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the vajra that summons through the samaya of all the tathāgatas, and this heart mantra of all the tathāgatas, called the samaya hook of all the tathāgatas, came forth from his own heart: hūṃ ḍakki jaḥ

Then, as soon as it had come forth, in all the clouds of the boundless ocean of world systems, all the lords of the three worlds, Maheśvara and the rest, however many there were, without exception and with none left out, surrounded by their hosts dwelling in all the worlds, were summoned by the samaya vajra hook of all the tathāgatas. They came to where the peaked pavilion made of vajra, gems, and jewels stood on the summit of Mount Meru, and took their places all around the Blessed One's peaked pavilion made of vajra, gems, and jewels.

Then Vajrapāṇi took the vajra from his own heart, and, brandishing it, he looked upon the circle of the three worlds, all those of the three realms however many there were, and declared: "Friends, enter the teaching of all the tathāgatas, and listen also to my command."

Then they said, "How should we practice?"

The Blessed One Vajrapāṇi said:

"Practice going for refuge
To the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha.
In order to attain all-knowing wisdom,
Friends, you must practice."

Then in this world system the Great God (Śiva), lord of all the three worlds, who had become lord of all the three worlds, grew arrogant, displayed great wrath, and spoke thus: "Listen, yakṣa! I am the chief of all the three worlds, Maheśvara, the creator and destroyer, lord of all bhūtas, the great god even of gods. Why should I listen to the words of you, a yakṣa?"

Then again Vajrapāṇi, brandishing the vajra, declared: "You wicked being, swiftly enter the maṇḍala and abide properly in my samaya!"

Then the great god of gods said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, who is this being who speaks thus to Īśvara?"

Then the Blessed One called all the hosts of the three worlds, Maheśvara and the rest, and spoke thus:

"Friends, go for refuge to the Three Jewels and practice the samaya and the vow. This Vajrapāṇi is a great bodhisattva who is a fierce, savage, wrathful yakṣa. Beware, lest he destroy all these three realms with his blazing vajra."

Then Maheśvara, who had become the lord of all the three worlds, together with his great retinue, by the power of his own knowledge, in order to frighten the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, sent forth a great blazing form, utterly terrifying, as a great and furious wrathful one, and displaying great savagery with peals of laughter like the neighing of horses, he spoke thus: "Listen! I am the lord of all the three worlds. Why do you give orders to me?"

Then Vajrapāṇi, proudly brandishing the vajra and smiling, spoke thus: "Listen, you of foul body, eater of human flesh, who eats the ashes of burned corpses as food and makes your food, your bed, and your clothing from them as well: do as I command!"

Then the Great God Maheśvara blessed all the three worlds and the realms of space to be overwhelmed by wrath, by great wrath, and spoke thus: "You too, listen to his command and take up the samaya!"

Then Vajrapāṇi, the great king of the wrathful ones, said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, this god called the Great God, arrogant through the power of his own knowledge and by the might of Maheśvara, will not bow to the teaching of all the tathāgatas. What is fitting to do with him?"

Then the Blessed One called to mind the great vajra samaya that had come forth from the heart of all the tathāgatas: oṃ ni sumbha vajra hūṃ phaṭ

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi pronounced this vajra heart mantra: hūṃ

Then, as soon as he had pronounced it, all the lords of the three worlds, the Great God and the rest, who had gathered from all the three realms, fell flat on their faces, uttering cries of distress, and went for refuge to the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi. The god called the Great God fell to the ground, lost consciousness, and died.

Then the Blessed One, knowing full well, said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Vajrapāṇi, grant refuge to this entire circle of the three worlds, and do not kill them."

Then Vajrapāṇi, the great king of the wrathful ones, having heard what the Blessed One had said, called all the gods and spoke thus: "If you want your lives, go for refuge to the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha, and listen also to my command."

Then they said, "We go for refuge to the Buddha, the Dharma, and the Saṅgha, together with the hosts of the mudrās. But we do not understand the command that you have taught."

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Vairocana, called them and declared: "Listen, gods! This is the lord of us and of all the tathāgatas, the father of all the tathāgatas, the one who carries out the command of all the tathāgatas, the eldest son of all the tathāgatas: the Blessed One, the bodhisattva, the great being Samantabhadra. In order to accomplish the aim of taming all beings, he has been empowered as the great king of the wrathful ones. Why is that? Because among you too there are wicked beings, such as the Great God, and since even all the tathāgatas, acting peacefully, cannot turn them back from evil, he has been blessed in order to subdue those evil beings. Therefore you must abide in his samaya." So he declared.

They said, "Blessed One, please protect us from losing our lives. We too will do as you have commanded."

The Blessed One declared, "Listen, friends! Go for refuge to this very one. It is this one who protects you completely, and no other."

Then those lords of the three states of existence, gathered from all the three realms, looked over toward where the Blessed One Vajradhara was, and with one voice raised a great cry of distress and said, "Blessed One, please protect us completely from this suffering of death! Sugata, please protect us completely!"

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi called those gods and the rest and declared: "Listen, you savage ones! Enter my teaching, lest this blazing vajra blaze up all at once and reduce you all to ashes."

They said, "You are the Blessed One Samantabhadra, the awakening of mind of all the tathāgatas, peaceful and disciplined, wishing to benefit all beings, granting fearlessness to all beings. Why then would the Blessed One burn us?"

Then Vajrapāṇi, the great king of the wrathful ones, declared to them: "Listen, friends! Because I carry out the command of all the tathāgatas, I am Samantabhadra. In order to purify the evil minds of beings like you, of savage disposition, if you do not abide in my samaya, I shall destroy you."

They said, "We shall do so."

Then Vajrapāṇi, the great king of the wrathful ones, in order to revive and raise up the other gods, apart from Maheśvara, spoke this heart mantra of all the tathāgatas called the vajra raising: oṃ vajrottiṣṭha

Then, as soon as he had spoken it, all those lords of the three states of existence gathered from the three realms, together with their retinues, apart from Maheśvara, recovered their minds from their swoon and breathed again. Experiencing the bliss of the gods, free of fear, dread, and bristling hair, they looked at the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi and rose up.

Then the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Great being, if this Great God, lord of the gods, is not raised up, his life will be wasted, and what good is that? If he is revived, he too will become a holy being."

Then Vajrapāṇi, thinking, "I shall do so," spoke this heart mantra for reviving the dead: vajra āyuḥ

As soon as he had spoken it, the Great God, lord of the gods, was revived from death; but though he wished to rise, he was unable to rise. Then he said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, are we to be tamed in such a way?"

The Blessed One said, "Since you will not consent to obey the command of this great being, this is precisely the taming for you, is it not? So it is."

Maheśvara said, "Blessed One, will you not act to protect me from that ferocious being?"

The Blessed One said, "I cannot protect you from him."

He asked, "Why is that?"

He said, "Because he is the lord of all the tathāgatas."

He said, "I do not understand the meaning of what the Blessed One has said. Why? Since I know of no lord of the three realms other than the tathāgatas, who is this?"

Then the Blessed One, the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, called again to the Great God and said, "O ferocious being, will you not obey my command?"

Then the Great God, hearing the words of Vajrasattva, became agitated and furious, and even while lying felled like that, he displayed a form of great terror and said, "I can bear to die, but I will not obey your word."

Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi displayed intense wrath, and from the soles of his own feet there emerged this attendant: oṃ patakariṣaṇa vajra hūṃ

Then from the soles of the Blessed One's feet there emerged the all-blazing vajra essence, frowning in wrath, its fangs bared, a great-faced vajra attendant, and it remained before the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, receiving his commands.

Then Vajrapāṇi, in order to purify Maheśvara, said this: oṃ patakariṣaṇa vajra hūṃ oṃ patakariśa sarva vajradhar anucarakata vajra hūṃ jaḥ

When he had said this, the Great God, together with the goddess Umā, was dragged out naked with his feet turned upward, while all the people laughed. The attendant who drags by the feet hauled them in and set them before the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, at his feet.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, what shall I do with this ferocious being and his followers?"

The Blessed One said: oṃ vajrakramaho

When he had said this, the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi pressed down the Great God with his left foot and pressed down upon the goddess Umā with his right foot, and spoke this heart mantra of his own: oṃ vajra āveśa hana pataṃ traṭ

As soon as he had spoken it, the Great God was possessed, and with his own thousand hands he beat his own thousand faces. Then, outside the tiered pavilion of the vajra, jewel and gem peak, built of vajras, jewels and gems, all the beings of the three states of existence raised a great cry: "That great being has tamed our lord!"

Then the Blessed One, moved by great compassion for the Great God, spoke this heart of loving-kindness of all the buddhas: oṃ buddha maitri vajra rakṣa haṃ

As soon as he had spoken it, all the suffering that had befallen the Great God was stilled. Then again, through the touch of the soles of Vajrapāṇi's feet, he attained the unsurpassed empowerment of accomplishment, the samādhis, the liberations, the dhāraṇīs, the wisdoms and the superknowledges, and so on, up to the point of being accomplished as a tathāgata. Then the Great God, touched by the soles of the feet of the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, experienced the bliss of the samādhi, dhāraṇī and liberation of all the tathāgatas. He offered up the body of the Great God at the feet of Vajrapāṇi, and below, beyond as many world systems as there are atoms in world systems as numerous as the grains of sand in thirty-two Ganges rivers, in the world system called Bhasmacchannā, Covered with Ash, he was born as the tathāgata named Bhasmeśvaranirghoṣa, Voice of the Lord of Ash.

Then from the body of the Great God came forth this inspired utterance:
How wonderful! The unsurpassed buddha wisdom
Of all the buddhas,
Having pressed one down with a host of syllables,
Sets one in nirvāṇa.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to all the others, Mahābala and the rest, the lords of the three worlds, "Friends, enter this great maṇḍala of the vajra samaya of all the tathāgatas! And once you have entered, keep the samaya of all the tathāgatas!"

They replied, "We shall do just as you command."

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi summoned those who had gathered from the three states of existence and said, "Friends, take up also the going for refuge to the Three, the training, the samaya and the vow, and abide in my samaya as well!"

They said, "If that is how it is, we have not understood the Blessed One's command and samaya."

Then Vajrapāṇi gave them this samaya of his own:
Having generated the awakening mind
In due order, after that
One should strive, as far as one is able,
In deep meditative equipoise for great awakening.

Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi had those gods and the others bind the mudrā of the great samaya of entry and so forth, and had them bind it with this heart mantra of the mudrā of the great samaya:

oṃ vajra samaya gṛhṇa stvaṃ bandha samya vajra satva samaya manusmara sarva tathāgata samaya stvaṃ dṛḍho me bhava sthiro me bhava āhāryo me bhava apratihāryo me bhava sarva karma su ca me cittaṃ śreyaḥ kuru hūṃ ha ha ha ha hoḥ

As soon as he had spoken it, the mudrā of Vajracaṇḍa Tirintiri descended into the hands of all the beings of the three states of existence of the three realms who had gathered there, and they were bound fast. Then Vajrapāṇi led them in according to the rite, showed them all the great maṇḍalas in their proper order, and empowered them with the empowerment of the vajra jewel. He also placed vajra emblems in their hands and empowered them with the empowerment of vajra names, and then all the tathāgatas set them to work for the benefit of sentient beings.

Then there were the lords of the three worlds, namely these. Maheśvara became Krodhavajra, Vajra of Wrath. Mahābala became Māyāvajra, Vajra of Illusion. Karṇika, the Youth of Sound, became Vajraghaṇṭa, Vajra Bell. Brahmā became Maunavajra, Vajra of the Sage. Indra became Vajrāyudha, Vajra Weapon. These and the others were empowered under the name of vidyārājas, kings of knowledge.

Then what was bestowed on all the lords of the gods who move in the atmosphere was this. The Sun became Vajrakuṇḍalī, the Coiled Vajra. The Moon became Vajraprabha, Vajra Light. The Lord of the Great Staff became Vajradaṇḍa, Vajra Staff. Piṅgala became Vajrapiṅgala. These and the others were empowered under the name of Vajracaṇḍas.

Then what was bestowed on all the lords of the gods who move in the sky was this. Madhumatta, Drunk on Honey, became Vajra Elephant Trunk. Madhukara, Honey Maker, became Vajramāla, Vajra Garland. The King became Vajravaśī, Vajra Power. Jayakara, Maker of Victory, became Vijayavajra, Vajra of Victory. These and the others were empowered under the name of gaṇapatis, lords of the hosts.

Then what was bestowed on all the lords of the earth's surface was this. Koṣapāla, Guardian of the Treasury, became Vajra Hammer. Agni became Vajrānala, Vajra Fire. Vāyu became Vajrānila, Vajra Wind. Kubera became Vajrabhairava, Vajra Terror. These and the others were empowered under the name of vajra messengers.

Then what was bestowed on all the lords, the gods beneath the earth, was this. Varāha, the Boar, became Vajrāṅkuśa, Vajra Hook. Yama became Vajrakāla, Vajra Time. Earth-Crest became Vajravināyaka, Vajra Lord of Obstructors. Varuṇa became Nāgavajra, Nāga Vajra. These and the others were empowered under the name of vajra servants.

Then all the goddesses of the lords of the three worlds were empowered with the empowerment of the vajra jewel. Their own emblems were blessed as vajras, they were empowered with the empowerment of vajra names, and they were set to work by all the tathāgatas for the benefit of sentient beings, namely as follows. Umā became Krodhavajrā, Wrathful Vajra Lady. The Golden Lady became Vajra Gold. She of the Six became Vajrakumārī, Vajra Maiden. Brahmāṇī became Vajraśāntā, Peaceful Vajra Lady. Indrāṇī became Vajramuṣṭi, Vajra Fist Lady. These and the others were empowered as vajra queens.

Then what was bestowed on all the women who move in the sky was this. Amṛtā, the Deathless, became Vajrāmṛtā, Deathless Vajra Lady. Rohiṇa became Vajra Radiance. Daṇḍadhāriṇī, Holder of the Staff, became Supreme Vajra Staff Lady. Jātāpahāriṇī, Stealer of Births, became Vajramekhalā, Vajra Girdle Lady. These and the others were empowered under the name of wrathful women.

Then what was bestowed on all the women who move in the atmosphere was this. The Slayer became Vajra Seizer. The Eater became Vajra Deep Lady. The Clothed One became Vajra Clothed Lady. Joy became Vajrahāsa. These and the others were empowered as mistresses of the hosts.

Then what was bestowed on all the women who move on the earth was this. Śivā became Vajradūtī, Vajra Messenger Woman. Vāyavī became Vajravegā, Swift Vajra Lady. Āgneyī became Vajrajvālā, Blazing Vajra Lady. Kauberī became Vajra Attendant Lady. These and the others were empowered as vajra messenger women.

Then what was bestowed on all the women who dwell beneath the earth was this. Vārāhī, the Sow, became Vajramukhī, Vajra Mouth. Cāmuṇḍī became Vajrakālī, Vajra Time Lady. Great Light became Vajra Putrid Lady. Vāruṇī became Vajramakarī, Vajra Sea-Monster Lady. These and the others were empowered as vajra maidservants.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi generated the wisdoms of the buddhas in all those who had entered, taught them all the bindings of the mudrās, and gave them this heart mantra of the oath of the vajra samaya:
This vajra is the great vajra,
Blessed by all the buddhas.
If you transgress this samaya,
It will swiftly burn your families to ash.
oṃ hana samaya hūṃ phaḍ

Then he gave this mudrā for seizing the heart:
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
And binding the index fingers as hooks,
Twist them, and twist them back again.
Before whoever thinks of an aim that he needs,
You must be present
So that what is needed is accomplished.
Otherwise that samaya
Will surely bring your lives to ruin.
Binding the vajra bond tightly,
Bend the index fingers and press them down firmly;
Joining the tips of both thumbs,
Press both middle fingers down firmly.
This is the vajra vidyādhara.
If anyone transgresses
The bound mudrā of samaya,
This samaya will kill you.
Binding the little fingers
Of both vajra mudrās,
Knot the index fingers behind the fingers,
Turn it back and place it on the head.
This is the samaya of the great-souled one.
Having bound the vajra vidyādhara,
Whoever one looks at in wrath
Will stand before one just so.
With the vajra of the clasped vajra,
Binding the protection fast and pressing it down:
That is the samaya of protection,
Which protects all sentient beings.

In order to protect someone,
Having bound the wrathful one,
You are set to remain at the back
Of whichever being is to be guarded.
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
Bind the index finger of the left vajra
With the little finger;
Twist it, twist it again, and release the net.
Having joined that samaya,
Which is wrathful equipoise,
You shall be present before him
For the supreme of all siddhis.
In the joined vajra binding,
Join both index fingers tightly;
Binding the two tips of thumb and middle finger
Into the vajra mudrā,
Turn it back and place it on the forehead.
If you wish to live,
Before whoever summons you
Always be present.

Then this is the heart mantra for holding the samaya of the samaya mudrā that holds the hearts of all the three worlds without exception: oṃ balitod balita vajra ākarṣaya hūṃ ja ja

This is the heart mantra for summoning the gods with the mudrā of the twisting vajra.
oṃ vajra agradhipaya samaye hūṃ This is for those who move in the atmosphere.
oṃ vajra māla agra vaṃ This is for the garland-bearers.
oṃ vajra bandha haṃ This is for those who move on the earth.
oṃ vajra pātāla bhañja bhañja hūṃ phaṭ This is for those who dwell beneath the earth.
oṃ heruka vajra samaya sarva duṣṭa samaya mudrā bhañjaka hūṃ phaḍ This is for the women.

Then the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, since all the tathāgatas have empowered me as the tamer of the ferocious, please explain well how I should deal with all those ferocious ones of the bound maṇḍalas."

When the Blessed One had heard this, he said: oṃ vajra sumbhani sumbha hūṃ phaṭ

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, in order to protect all sentient beings, spoke this heart mantra for summoning all the maṇḍalas: oṃ vajra samya ākarṣaya sarva maṇḍala vajradhāra satya mātikrama hūṃ phaṭ
Binding both with vajra hooks,
Circle them around the heart.
The two index fingers as hooks
Are the supreme summoning of the outer maṇḍalas.

As soon as he had explained this, all the maṇḍalas settled, encircling the outside of the summit of Mount Meru. Then the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi called all those assembled maṇḍalas and said, "O friends, give up killing and devote yourselves to taking up the samaya."

Then those beings of the samaya of the outer maṇḍala said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, we are ferocious beings who eat flesh, and our life depends on stealing vitality. Blessed One, tell us how we are to live."

Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this Ferocious Vajracaṇḍa: oṃ duṣṭa vajra krodha hana daha paca vidhvanasya vikari sarva duṣṭa samaya mudrā maṇḍal bhañja bhañja mardha mardha khāda khāda bar mantra vajra samaya hūṃ phaṭ
The vajra wrathful index fingers, properly
Bringing the nails together and twining them,
And joining the two tips tightly:
That mudrā crushes the ferocious.

As soon as he had spoken it, all the ferocious maṇḍalas, gathered into one, were tormented and torn apart by many, and their bound samaya mudrās were torn apart. Those beings of the ferocious samaya also burned and were thoroughly cooked, so that they raised great cries and sounds of distress, and, joining their palms toward where the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, was, they said, "Blessed One, please protect us, so that we are not parted from our lives."

Then Vajrapāṇi again said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, please tell me what I should do with those ferocious maṇḍalas."

Then the Blessed One said: oṃ nisumbha hana daha paca gṛhṇa bandha hūṃ phaṭ

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this unbearable Vajracaṇḍa: oṃ mahā vajra khura krodha vataya sarva duṣṭa maṇḍalaṃ vināśaya sarva duṣṭa samyaṃ bhigakira vidhvansaya sphoṭaya bhañjaya sarva duṣṭa samaya mudrā bandha gṛhṇa hana daha paca sarva duṣṭa samaya satvaṃ vajra samaya hūṃ phaṭ

As soon as he had spoken it, all the ferocious samayas, mudrās and maṇḍalas gathered into one and sank into the great ocean.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi again said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, since the Blessed One has commanded me, 'Tame all the ferocious ones,' what am I to do with all those grahas, the sorceresses, the grahas and the rest?"

Then the Blessed One said: oṃ hana hana vajra hūṃ phaṭ

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this heart mantra for summoning all the ferocious grahas, the sorceresses and the rest: oṃ vajra ākarṣaya śīghraṃ sarva duṣṭa grahaṃ vajradhara satvana hūṃ ja

As soon as he had spoken it, all the ferocious grahas, the sorceresses and the rest, settled, encircling the outside of the summit of Mount Meru. Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi called all the ferocious grahas, the sorceresses and the rest, and said, "O sirs, take up the training of giving up killing, and the samaya and the vow! Otherwise the vajra streaming with light will blaze up as one, and your families will surely be burned up without remainder!"

Then all the ferocious grahas, the sorceresses and the rest, joined their palms toward where the Blessed One was and said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, we are eaters of flesh. Please tell us what we are to do."

Then the Blessed One said to Vajrapāṇi, "Vajrapāṇi, generate great compassion for those beings and give them a means."

Then the great Vajrapāṇi spoke this mudrā and heart mantra for knowing the signs of death of all sentient beings: oṃ vajra pratigṛhṇa hṛdaya mākarṣaya yaddyayaṃ mato māmaddāradhenmiyate tadyasya hṛdayaṃ niskramitu samaya hūṃ jaḥ

Then the binding of its mudrā is explained.

At the heart, the two vajra fists
are bound together very firmly;
then with the tips of the vajra index fingers
one should rub one's own armpits.

"With that mudrā, you are to eat the heart-essence of all sentient beings."

Then all the ferocious grahas, the sorceresses and the rest, hearing that joyful word, leapt and bounded about and went off to their own abodes.

Then the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi again said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, since all the Tathāgatas have declared that the taming of the wicked is mine to do, Blessed One, please tell me what I am to do about these illnesses, fevers and the rest."

Then the Blessed One said, oṃ hūṃ phaṭ.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke the heart mantra for summoning the illnesses, fevers and the rest: oṃ vajra samaya ānaya sarva duṣṭa jvara dhani nāśaya hūṃ phaṭ

As soon as he had spoken it, all the illnesses, fevers and the rest gathered round outside the summit of Mount Meru and stayed there. Then Vajrapāṇi called the illnesses, fevers and the rest and spoke these words: "O good sirs, take up the vow that holds to the training of refraining from killing sentient beings."

They replied, "Blessed One, we live by stealing the vital luster of sentient beings. Blessed One, please tell us properly what we are to do."

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi spoke this heart mantra of the wisdom mudrā by which one's own actions become purified: oṃ vajra karma viśodhaya sarva āparṇāni buddha sattyena samaya hūṃ

Then the binding of the mudrā is explained.

Firmly joining the vajra palms,
bending both index fingers,
and placing the thumbs together, even,
one presses: this is the stealer of sin.

"When one whom any of the illnesses, fevers and the rest have struck performs the rite and shows that mudrā, you must let him go; otherwise you will be parted from your lives."

Then they said, "We shall do so," and went back.

Then the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to all the Tathāgatas, "Blessed Ones, since I came forth from your own hearts in order to remove all obscurations and to purify completely all the obscurations of karma, then, Blessed One, please tell me what I am to do about all those beings of the lower realms, the hells and the rest."

The Blessed One said, oṃ kṣapaya vajra svāhā.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, spoke this heart mantra for summoning all the wheels of the lower destinies, Raurava and the rest: oṃ sarva avāya ākarṣaṇa viśodhana vajra samya hūṃ phaṭ

As soon as he had spoken it, those dwelling in the lower realms, Raurava, Mahāraurava and the rest, gathered round outside the summit of Mount Meru and stayed there. Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi called all the sentient beings who had fallen into the lower realms and spoke these words: "O good sirs, go for refuge to the Three Jewels, and take up the samaya, the vow, and the mudrā and heart mantra of liberation from all the lower realms."

Then with one voice they said these words: "Blessed One, please protect us from this torment of suffering. We go for refuge to the Buddha, to the Dharma, to the Saṅgha, and to you."

Then Vajrapāṇi spoke this heart mantra called Destroyer of All Lower Realms: oṃ vajrapāṇi visphoṭaya sarva apāya bhandhanāni pramokṣaya sarvāpāya gatebhyaḥ sarva satvaṃ sarva tathāgata vajra samaya trāṭ

Then the binding of its mudrā is explained.

Firmly forming the vajra binding,
joining both the middle fingers,
the last four are joined at their tips:
this is known as the destroyer of sin.

Then all the sentient beings dwelling in the wheel of the three lower realms, on seeing that mudrā of Vajrapāṇi, died and passed from all the lower realms and were born at the feet of the Blessed One Vairocana. Those lower realms were cast into the great ocean.

Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, the Blessed One, all the Tathāgatas, gave me the vajra of accomplishment of all the Tathāgatas, saying, 'Seek the means, from bringing refuge, benefit and every happiness to the realm of sentient beings, entire and without remainder, up to bringing them to attain the supreme fruit of accomplishment of all the Tathāgatas.' Since all the Tathāgatas have so commanded, may all the Tathāgatas tell me what those people are to do."

All the Tathāgatas then gathered as one and declared: oṃ vajrapāṇi mahāmanḍalaṃ praveśaya sarva duṣṭa rodranni varya pravevya pramaśaya trura dṛṣṭa paryva paṃ maṃ viśodhaya nāśaya vināśaya ha ha ha ha hūṃ

Then, having heard those words declared by all the Tathāgatas, in order to protect the realm of sentient beings, entire and without remainder, and to bring them benefit and every happiness, from the supreme accomplishment up to the attainment of the fruit of the wisdom and superknowledges of all the Tathāgatas, Vajrapāṇi taught this great maṇḍala called Victorious over the Three Worlds, arisen from the great vajra samaya of all the Tathāgatas.

Now I shall fully explain
the supreme great maṇḍala,
like the Vajradhātu,
the best, which brings every accomplishment,
called Victorious over the Three Worlds,
arisen from the vajra samaya,
which brings forth the awakening of the buddhas
and utterly destroys all the wicked.

For it, the cord should be drawn with this mantra: oṃ vajra samaya sūtraṃ mātikrama ho

Make it square, with four gates,
beautifully adorned with four archways,
furnished with four cords,
and richly adorned with silks and garlands.
At the corners of the whole maṇḍala
and inside the frames of the gates,
adorn it with vajras and jewels.
Lay the cords of the maṇḍala's outer tier.
Within it, the one of wisdom
makes a square with four gates,
with eight pillars and archways.
In the supreme place of the vajra pillars
it is adorned with five maṇḍalas.
Lay the cords of the maṇḍala in this way.
Once the cords are laid, fill it with colors.

For that, the recitation over the colors is this.

oṃ vajra citra samaya hūṃ

Then, seated in the center,
the vajra master, in meditative equipoise,
should open with the mind
the four vajra gates.

For that, the mantra for opening the gates is this: oṃ vajrodghaṭya samaya praveśaya hūṃ

On a square slab made of gold or silver,
or else of clay, well drawn,
one should set
an image of the Buddha.

For that, the heart mantra for inviting all the Tathāgatas is this: oṃ vajra jvāla agni pradrīpta ākarṣaya sarva tathāgatānā mahāvajra samaye hūṃ jaḥ

Before the Buddha, a vajra
should be placed, encircled with flames;
likewise a jewel ringed with light,
and the lotus and the vajra, all in the same way.

Then this is the heart mantra for setting out the vajra samaya mudrās: oṃ satva vajrā jvāla māla hūṃ phaḍ oṃ ratna vajrī jvāla māla huṃ trāṃ oṃ dharma vajrī jvāla māla hūṃ hrīḥ oṃ karma vajrā jvāla māla hūṃ aḥ

Having stepped across with the vajra stride,
likewise before the Buddha,
the knowing one draws according to the rite:
this is the maṇḍala called Vajra hūṃ.

For that, the heart mantra of the vajra stride is this: oṃ vajra vegākrama hūṃ

In the same way, in all the maṇḍalas, the Vajradhātu maṇḍala and the rest, one should step across the cords and move about everywhere.

Then its essential point is explained.

Lifting the lines with the mind,
whether they are vajra cords or anything else,
though one goes out or comes in,
one does not thereby break the samaya.

In its center, the great being
Vajrapāṇi is to be drawn,
the gleaming color of a great blue utpala,
embodied as the one called Vajra hūṃ,
his mouth with fangs slightly bared,
his face smiling yet wrathful,
standing with one leg stretched and one drawn in,
his light wreathed in a garland of flames.
With the sole of his left foot
he is to press down Maheśvara (Śiva);
with his right foot, upon the breasts
of Umā, draw him pressing down.

Its heart mantra is this: hūṃ

In all the directions around him,
the vajra wrathful ones are placed,
baring their fangs in wrath,
their light wreathed in garlands of flame.

Then their heart mantras are these: oṃ vajra satva krodha hūṃ phaḍ oṃ vajra krodha āveśaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra karma krodha rāgaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra duṣṭi krodha sādhu sādhu hūṃ phaṭ

In the second supreme maṇḍala,
having stepped across with the vajra stride,
there draw the Vajra Empowerment,
surrounded by the vajra wrathful ones.

Then their heart mantras are explained: traṃ oṃ vajra bhṛkuḍī krodha hara hara huṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra sūrya mahā jvala māla krodha jvalaya sarvaṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra krodha ketu dehi hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra hāsa krodha ha ha ha ha hūṃ phaṭ

In the third supreme maṇḍala,
having stepped across with the vajra stride,
one should draw the Vajra Host,
surrounded by the great-souled wrathful ones.

Then their heart mantras are explained: hrīḥ oṃ vajra dharma krodha vināśaya vīśodhaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra tīkṣṇa krodha cchinda cchinda hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra hetu krodha praveśaya maṇḍale sarvaṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra krodha bhāṣa vata vata hūṃ phaṭ

In the fourth supreme maṇḍala,
having stepped across with the vajra stride,
one should draw the Vajra Descent,
surrounded by the host of vajra wrathful ones.

For that, their heart mantras are explained: aḥ hūṃ vajra karma hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra kavaca krodha rakṣa hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra yakṣa krodha khādā khada hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra krodha muṣṭi sādhaya samaya hūṃ phaṭ

In the corners of the maṇḍala,
following the manner of the Vajradhātu,
each in its proper sequence,
draw all the secret offerings.

Then these are the heart mantras of their mudrās: oṃ vajra rāsye rāgaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra māle abhiṣiñca hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra gīte gada gada hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajri nṛdye vaśi kuru hūṃ phaṭ

Outside the supreme maṇḍala,
having stepped across with the vajra stride,
in its very corners
place the four offering goddesses.

Then their heart mantras are these: oṃ vajra dhupe pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra buṣpa pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra āloke pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra gandhe pūjā spharaṇa samaye hūṃ phaṭ

In the middle of the four gates
one should place the Hook and the rest;
in the places outside the maṇḍala,
the outer vajra families.

Then their heart mantras are explained: oṃ vajra aṃkuśa mahā krodha ākarṣaya sarva samayaṃ hūṃ jaḥ oṃ vajra pāśa mahā krodha prapeśaya sarva samaya hūṃ hūṃ oṃ vajra sphoṭa mahā krodha bandha bandha sarva samaya hūṃ vaṃ oṃ vajra āveśa mahā krodha āveśaya sarva samaye hūṃ aḥ

Then here the rite of the great maṇḍala of Victorious over the Three Worlds is explained in full.

For that, first of all, the vajra master himself should bind the mudrā of the vajra wrathful one Tirintiri and enter. Having entered, he should make this supplication to all the Tathāgatas: "Blessed Ones, I have come under the power of the wrath of all the Tathāgatas. Those who deserve to be subdued, I subdue. As for those who deserve to be gathered in, may the Blessed Ones tell me how I am to gather them in."

Having said this, he places the mudrā of the vajra wrathful one Tirintiri at his own heart, just as it is, and performs the rite with Vajrāṅkuśa and the rest. Then he should again bind the mudrās of all the samayas, and by that all of them will come. Then again he performs the four secret offerings, and incense and the rest likewise.

Then by this rite he should make the vajra disciple enter. The vajra master himself binds the mudrā of the vajra wrathful one Tirintiri, and he should have the disciple too bind it with these heart mantras: oṃ gṛhṇa vajra samaya hūṃ vaṃ

Then, having him put on blue clothing, binding his head with a blue crown-band and wrapping his face in blue silk, he should make him enter with this mantra: oṃ vajra samyaṃ praveśāme

Then, having made him enter, he should bring about the descent upon him by this mudrā and heart mantra of the samaya of vajra descent: oṃ vajra āveśa āḥ

Then, once it has descended, because it has descended in this way, all the Tathāgatas bless him.

He knows in an instant all of the past, the future and the present. He also becomes free of harm. He becomes invincible to all of them. And by hūṃ he becomes able both to subdue and to benefit all sentient beings. Vajrapāṇi, too, constantly accomplishes all his purposes.

Then give him the heart mantra of the oath. Then remove the covering from his face and show him the great maṇḍala just as it is. Merely by seeing the great maṇḍala he is freed from all sins. He becomes able to be victorious over the three worlds in their entirety. By hūṃ he becomes able to summon, to make enter, to bind, to bring under control and to crush all the gods, the Great God (Śiva) and the rest. And through the blessing of all the Tathāgatas, the Blessed One, the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, constantly follows him and bestows his own accomplishments.

Then, empowering him with the vajra empowerment, he gives into his two hands his own emblem, the sharp vajra, just as it is, with this mantra: oṃ vajrapāṇi vajra karma karod bhava

Then with this mantra he confers the vajra name empowerment: vajra krodha tvaṃ abhiṣiñcāmi vajra nāma abhiṣekata he vajra nāma

Then whatever name is given to anyone should be joined with the call "O."

Then the wisdoms are generated.

Placed in equipoise as the vajra wrathful one,
imagine at the heart the form of a vajra
whose light is many blazing flames:
it makes all people undergo the descent.
Placed in equipoise as the vajra wrathful one,
and likewise imagining
a vajra jewel at the forehead,
one brings all sentient beings under control.
Placed in equipoise as the vajra wrathful one,
imagine at the throat a vajra lotus
whose light is many blazing garlands:
it destroys all sentient beings.
Placed in equipoise as the vajra wrathful one,
place the universal vajra at the crown,
whose light is many blazing garlands:
it protects all those people.

Then the heart mantras of those wisdom mudrās are these: huṃ sarva vajra krodha āveśa aḥ ho ratna vajra krodha bhāṣa mānaya traṃ hūṃ vajra dharma krodha nāśaya phaṭ hūṃ karma vajra krodha rakṣa

Then the wisdom mudrā for summoning the gods is taught.

Having bound the hūṃ mudrā,
imagine the hand as a vajra hook;
crooking the index finger in wrath
becomes the supreme summoning of the gods.
Having drawn a vajra hook
in the middle of the sole of one's own foot,
if one constantly presses down with it,
the gods are summoned without fail.
The vajra hook mudrā itself,
well opened out and set in place,
when it is urged on, summons
all women, Umā and the rest.
Placing the vajra hook
likewise upon the index finger itself,
whatever of the gods it presses down,
that is the supreme summoning.

Their heart mantras are these: oṃ vajra krodha ākarṣaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra karma aṃkuśa krodha ākarṣayaṃ huṃ phaṭ o vajra krodha daṇḍa āgra ākarṣaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra krodha si gaccha ākarṣaya mite vajra samaya manusmara hūṃ phaṭ

Then the wisdom of the four kinds of mudrā is taught.

Standing with one leg stretched and one drawn in,
in the manner of the vajra descent,
by hūṃ alone, in an instant,
one makes all people undergo the descent.
The vajras have spoken well:
by that which has the four hūṃ syllables,
the heart of all the buddhas,
all are surely summoned.
Placed in equipoise as the vajra wrathful one,
through the supreme deity of vajra wrath
one slays all people:
this is the yoga of vajra hūṃ.
Donning armor on the body with the mind,
for oneself or for others,
by the application of the great armor
one protects all those people.

Then their heart mantras are these: oṃ vajra krodha kāya āveśaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra vidyottama mahā krodha ānaya ho bhagavān vajra hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vajra krodha tiṣṭha hana daha paca vināśaya hūṃ kareṇa pātaya vajra samaya hūṃ phaṭ oṃ mano dṛḍha vajra kavaca krodha rakṣa hūṃ phaḍ

Then the wisdom of the mudrā that seals all sentient beings.

Placed in equipoise as the vajra wrathful one,
by reciting vajra hūṃ,
whatever and whomever it enters
becomes sealed with the seal.
One should utter it in a great voice: ḍakki hūṃ phaḍ
Whomever one addresses once in wrath,
the blazing vajra draws him forth.
In the samādhi of the vajra wrathful one,
by looking with the vajra gaze,
whatever sentient beings there may be,
each and every one will die.
Having set the mind on the stillness of slaying,
one meditates on a vajra at the heart.
With hūṃ, by the mind,
one intends to set one's own seal.
This, whereby one sets the seal of the mind
on any sentient being by any means:
by this mudrā, most thoroughly,
one should accomplish all the rites.

Then their heart mantras are these: ṭakkia ṭakki ja ṭakki hūṃ ḍakki haṃ

With each mudrā, too, the four heart mantras of the mudrās are applied to the four activities.

Then the wisdom of the secret wrathful mudrā is taught.

Embracing with all the limbs,
one should direct hūṃ at the object;
having joined the two organs,
that one's life is ruined.
Joined with hūṃ,
meeting the lips and holding the lip with the teeth,
whoever fully joins the two organs,
that one becomes directly present.
Whoever makes the union with hūṃ,
that one experiences bliss.
If the two organs are joined,
whoever it is meets with suffering.
Fully applying hūṃ,
and having joined the two organs,
if one presses tight with all the limbs,
that one's body will be protected.

Then their heart mantras are these: hūṃ a hūṃ ja hūṃ ho hūṃ haṃ hūṃ he

Then the wisdom of the mudrā of the very samaya of the great maṇḍala of Victorious over the Three Worlds is taught.

Whoever, for the benefit of sentient beings,
in the manner of the Vajradhātu,
practices the recollection of the Buddha
will attain buddhahood.
Drawing Īśvara and Umā too
upon the ground, press them down in the same way;
following the sequence in which they were drawn,
one should bind the sattva mudrā.
Merely by binding it,
one is victorious over the three worlds,
and accomplishments and magical powers like those of Vajra hūṃ
are swiftly attained.
Just as it is drawn in the picture,
seated in the center, encircled by flames,
with body, speech and mind as vajra,
one should bind the sattva mudrā.

Then from these it is explained:
the vajra activity is unsurpassed.
By accomplishing the recollection of the Buddha
one swiftly attains buddhahood itself.
If one binds Victorious over the Three Worlds,
one becomes victorious over the three worlds.
One becomes a great vajra and goes everywhere,
like the one called Vajra hūṃ.
By the vajra empowerment one becomes a king;
possessing the good Dharma, one has power over the world.
The karma vajra, the great wrathful one,
performs the vajra activities.
The sattva, utterly steadfast as the wrathful one,
truly summons with the wrathful hook.
The wrathful one of passion makes others passionate;
the wrathful one of goodness bestows joy;
with the wrathful frown he makes all fierce;
the wrathful sun has supreme splendor;
the wrathful one of glory gathers in riches;
laughing ha ha, he acts as a māra;
the wrathful one of Dharma overpowers all dharmas;
he cuts with the vajra sword;
the wrathful one of cause steals away suffering;
the wrathful one of speech steals away words;
the wrathful one of karma excels in activities;
with protection he protects;
the wrathful yakṣa devours even enemies;
the wrathful fist bestows accomplishments.

Then the binding of the vajra samaya mudrās is explained.

It becomes the twofold vajra,
renowned as the supreme samaya.
I shall explain how they are bound:
the binding of the wrathful one is unsurpassed.
Making the fists into vajras,
bind the little fingers as a hook;
threatening with the two index fingers:
this is called Victorious over the Three Worlds.
Likewise, joining the sides of the index fingers,
curve them fully like a jewel.
Raise the middle fingers evenly to make a lotus;
threaten with both the middle and the index fingers.
Make both index fingers into a vajra;
with the right, as a hook, one should summon.
Knot that same one as hūṃ;
likewise for bestowing goodness.
Place two fingers at the furrow of the brow;
at the heart, circle the index finger as a sun.
Stretching out the hand, place it on the crown;
with the index finger at the mouth, act as if laughing.
Joining the nails of the index fingers,
with the right, the sword is bound.
Raising the middle and index fingers evenly,
draw them out from inside the mouth of the wheel;
make the index and middle fingers into a vajra;
the two index fingers are the great fangs;
the bent index finger is bound as a vajra.
Vajra lāsye and the rest,
with the mudrās of the female sattvas, that very hūṃ:
the Dharma mudrās so distinguished
go together with hūṃ.

Then the Dharma mudrās of the vajra samaya are explained.

hūṃ is of the Buddha Vajra;
tāṃ is of Vajragarbha;
hrī is of the Vajra Host;
a is of all the vajras.

hūṃ he traṃ taṃ hi hi deha bhik dhi hūṃ grā kripaṃ dṛ a

These fully accomplish the Dharma mudrās
of the many vajra wrathful ones.

Then the karma mudrās of the vajra samaya are explained.

To summarize the karma mudrās
of the great vajra family:
in the manner of the vajra swagger and the rest,
they are explained as the two wrathful fists.
Whatever the mudrās may be,
for whatever kind of activity they are to be done,
one should turn them to that purpose
and apply the mudrā accordingly.
This is the rite of all the mudrās.

Then here the binding of the general mudrā of the great maṇḍala of Victorious over the Three Worlds is explained.

The mudrā of Victorious over the Three Worlds
is the samaya of the vajra index fingers;
its mantra is vajra hūṃ;
by it all accomplishments are obtained in a moment.
Bind both little fingers as hooks,
above and below both vajras:
that samaya hook mudrā
summons all in an instant.
Of all the supreme vidyās
it is declared to be Victorious over the Three Worlds.
It harms all
and likewise performs all activities.
Setting the two vajra index fingers well
in full equipoise, so that they are even,
it raises up all the dead:
it is called the Vajra Raiser.
Binding both vajras well
with the vajra binding,
and placing it reversed upon the crown,
it increases life and freedom from illness.
Firmly forming the vajra binding,
evening the thumbs, insert them within;
crooking the index fingers firmly,
inserted, it summons at the feet.
Having bound Victorious over the Three Worlds,
whatever sentient being's image
one presses down well with the left foot,
that being is won over within half a month.
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
if one strikes them against each other,
upon whatever sentient being's body
it falls, that one too is struck.
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
make armor for oneself or for others;
if one binds it with the two index fingers,
one will surely be protected.
Binding the vajras at the palms,
one should cover them, wrathful in mind;
the thumbs firm as a vajra:
the great vajra family is accomplished.
Make Sattvavajra firm:
press the middle fingers with both thumbs;
the little fingers as the vajra tips,
sharp, hold the samaya.
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
crooking and binding the two index fingers,
twisting them and twisting them back
is the supreme summoning of the gods.
Firmly forming the vajra binding,
crooking the two index fingers, press hard;
by joining both thumbs,
the binding in the middle is that of the sky.
From the two vajra mudrās,
binding both little fingers,
linking the two index fingers back to back,
turning it about is that of the sky-goers.
Tightening the vajra binding,
one should bind it with the vajra protection:
this is the samaya of those of the earth;
it does all things and is hard to transgress.
Binding the two vajra mudrās,
on the left vajra index finger
the little finger embraces and constantly hooks:
this summons those beneath the earth.
Linking with the vajra binding,
both index fingers held firm,
the middle fingers and thumbs as a vajra:
this destroys the mudrās of the wicked.

Binding the two vajra mudrās
in equipoise, place them at the heart;
squeezing with the wrathful fist
destroys outer maṇḍalas.
Upon the left vajra index finger,
hold it with the crooked right index finger;
if, in fierce wrath, one strikes forcefully,
it makes even Mount Meru fall.
Upon the left vajra index finger,
hold with the right index finger as a hook;
by crooking it in fierce wrath,
one gains power over all the grahas.
Having made the vajra binding,
tighten the fists firmly at the heart;
by rubbing one's armpits with the vajra index fingers,
one gives the heart.
With the tips of the two index fingers,
at the base of the two thumbs,
one should squeeze, wrathful in mind:
supreme for summoning fevers and the rest.
Firmly joining the vajra palms,
bending both index fingers,
joining the thumbs fully even,
and pressing: this steals away sin.
Within the two vajra mudrās,
hiding the two index fingers,
and casting them well upward:
supreme for raising up the fallen.
Firmly forming the vajra binding,
joining the sides of both middle fingers,
and bringing together the sides of the last four,
in an instant it tears sin apart.

Then the binding of the mudrās for accomplishing the great maṇḍala of all the Tathāgatas is explained.

Holding with the vajra mudrā,
first lay the cords of the maṇḍala;
having laid the cords, then
laying the cords as they should be is explained.
With both vajra mudrās
fully joined at the tips of the index fingers,
one should then, with a blazing gaze,
summon all the colors.
Joining the vajra index fingers well,
bending them back and tightening them,
then opening them in fierce wrath:
this is the best for opening the gates.

Then the accomplishment of all the mudrās of the vajra families is explained.

Taking the posture with the left leg extended,
uttering the cry of the wrathful one,
if one looks with the wrathful gaze,
one accomplishes all the rites.

From the Great King of Rituals of the vajra samaya of all the Tathāgatas, the extensive rite of the great maṇḍala of Victorious over the Three Worlds is complete.

The fifth fascicle.

Then again the Blessed One entered into the samādhi called the vajra blessing arising from the vajra dhāraṇī samaya of all the Tathāgatas, and spoke this supreme vidyā of his own: oṃ sarva vajriṇi vajra māte ānaya sarva vajra satyena hūṃ ja

Then, as soon as it was spoken, from the heart of Vajrapāṇi the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi himself, the lord of the net of Vajrapāṇis, his whole body ablaze, came forth transformed into the goddesses of the mudrās of the secret samaya of the vajra wrathful ones. Having accomplished the aims of all the Tathāgatas in all the world realms, in order to keep secret the consorts of the Blessed One Vajrasattva, they took the forms of the vajra mudrās of body, speech and mind, and, sitting on moon disks in the manner of the great maṇḍala of Victorious over the Three Worlds of the Blessed One Vairocana, they uttered this inspired utterance:

How wonderful! The unsurpassed
secret wisdom of all the buddhas,
for the bliss of the Tathāgatas,
has become the consorts themselves.
hrīḥ

Then again Vajrapāṇi spoke this maṇḍala of the samaya mudrās of his own family, called the Vajra Secret Samaya.

Now I shall fully explain
the supreme vajra maṇḍala;
because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
it is called the Secret of the Wrathful Ones.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
lay the cords of all the maṇḍalas.
In the places of the five maṇḍalas
one should set the secret mudrās.
In the center of the vajra maṇḍala
one should set the images of the buddhas.
The samaya of the buddha wrathful one, too,
the skilled one draws as it should be.
Having stepped across with the vajra stride,
in that maṇḍala of Vajrapāṇi
draw at the center a spear point with lips,
and firmly set a vajra there;
draw it in the middle of blazing light.
In their proper sequence:
the vajra, the vajra hook,
the arrow, and Joy likewise.
Having stepped across with the vajra stride,
in the second supreme maṇḍala also
one should draw a vajra jewel
dwelling in the center of a wheel.
Draw a vajra in the middle of a frown;
likewise the vajra sun and banner,
and likewise its garland of teeth:
one should draw them in actual vajra form.
Having stepped across with the vajra stride,
in the third supreme maṇḍala also
draw the vajra lotus, the divine emblem,
dwelling in the center of a lotus.
Draw a lotus in the middle of flames;
the sword and the wheel likewise.
The vajra tongue too, as is proper,
is always set at its side.
Having stepped across with the vajra stride,
in the fourth supreme maṇḍala also
draw a vajra upon a vajra laid flat,
encircled by great vajra light.
In all its directions
is light wreathed in every kind of flame:
the universal vajra, the supreme armor,
the vajra fangs, the supreme fist.
In the corners and the outer places
one should draw them as is proper.

Now I shall fully explain,
summarizing the mudrā vidyās:

jhiḥ oṃ vajra krodhe samaye jhiḥ oṃ vajra krodhe roṣaṃkuśavanaya sarvana jhiḥ oṃ vajra roṣe kāma vajriṇi vaśamme ānaya hi jhiḥ oṃ vajra duṣṭa krodhe toṣaya sarvana jhiḥ

This is the maṇḍala called Vajra jhiḥ.

jiḥ oṃ vajra bhṛkuśī krodhe hara sarva arthana jiḥ oṃ vajra jvāla mālā praveśa mahā krodha agni jvālaya sarva nara viroṣe jiḥ oṃ vajra dhvaja agre keyūra mahā krodhe dehi me sarva arthana jiḥ oṃ vajra aṭṭāhāsani hāsa hāsa aṭṭātṭe hāsena māraya jiḥ

This is the maṇḍala called Vajra jiḥ.

diḥ oṃ vajra śuddha krodhe hana hana māraya duṣṭana diḥ oṃ vajra tīkṣṇa krodhe cchinda vajra koṣena sarvani deḥ oṃ vajra hetu mahā krodhe praveśa cakra praveśaya sarvan diḥ oṃ vajra jihā mahā krodhe vaśe vaca muñca diḥ

This is the maṇḍala called Vajra diḥ.

hrīḥ oṃ vajra sarvan mukhe karma vajraṇi mahā krodhe kuru kuru sarvana hrīḥ oṃ vajra kavac krodhe rakṣa mama hiḥ oṃ vajra caḍṇa krodhe mahā yakṣini vajra daṃṣṭa kārāla bhīṣne paya hiḥ oṃ vajra krodhe muṣḍi bandha hiḥ

This is the maṇḍala called Vajra hiḥ.

Then, for the corners of the maṇḍala, he spoke the heart-mantras of the vidyās of the secret offerings, Vajranṛtyā and the rest:
oṃ vajra hūṃ khe vajra hūṃ ghāṃ vajra hūṃ te vajra hūṃ tvaṃ

For the outer corners one should recite the heart-mantras of the cymbal offerings:
oṃ vajra trite vajra ḍaṃḍa vajra dhātu vajra ḍaummudha

Then he spoke the heart-mantras of the offerings of the gatekeepers:
vajra jaḥ jaḥ vajra hūṃ hūṃ vajra vaṃ vaṃ vajra aḥ aḥ

Next, this extensive rite of entry into the secret maṇḍala of the vajra family is explained. First of all, one should have the disciple enter in accordance with the words for entering the great maṇḍala of Victorious over the Three Worlds. Then, in order to make offering to Vajradhara, one should make offering with dance, by means of the samaya mudrās of the vajra family of the vajra secret, the counterpart mudrās, the subsidiary mudrās, and the wisdom mudrās.

Here, this is the wisdom of the mudrās of offering by means of the dance, the responding dance, the subsidiary dance, and the wisdom dance. First of all, singing the vajra song with the heart-mantra of the Vajradhātu compendium, and making offering by praising all the tathāgatas, the vajra master should forcefully snap the vajra mudrā of Sattvavajrī. Then the mudrās should be made to descend properly upon all who are present.

In the manner of the vajra dance,
The two fingers of Vajracaṇḍa,
In the mudrā made by Vajra Hūṃ,
Should be bound firmly at the heart.
Then, by the rite of the dance,
With the Hook of Vajracaṇḍa
Invite all the buddhas.
Let the vajra arrow be shot.
And having shot the vajra arrow,
Accomplish it with Vajra Joy.
Releasing the mudrā according to the rite,
Clap the palms and bind them.
By this rite of offering
Vajrapāṇi is pleased,
And once he is pleased, all one's aims
Are accomplished at will in an instant.

Here the heart-mantras of the dance mudrās are these:
sidhya vajra ānaya vajra rāgaya vajra sādhu vajra

After that, one should perform the offering of the responding dance.

Likewise, the left hand of the dance
Is seized by the right fist,
And, turning it, with the face of the fingers
One should place it on the forehead.
Likewise, the dancer, the sun too
Having circled, should properly summon.
Raising the vajra banner,
Laughing with vajra ha ha,
By the rite of this offering,
The king and all other people
Will gratify one with power, supreme splendor,
Gifts, and all that one needs.

Here these are the heart-mantras of the responding mudrās:
ahi vajra jvālaya vajra dehi vajra hāsa hāsa vajra

Likewise, releasing the dance,
With the joined lotus bud
Place the fingertips at the heart,
And bow with a heart that is moved.
Likewise, the dancer with the vajra
Acts as if cutting obstructors with a sword.
Whirling a wheel of firebrand,
Circle in the manner of a wheel maṇḍala.
As if singing in vajra words,
Make offering to Vajrapāṇi.
By the rite of this offering
All things become certain.

Here these are the heart-mantras of the subsidiary mudrās:
gāmaya vajra cchindaya vajra brāhmiya vajra bruhi vajra

Bending the fingers of Vajracaṇḍa properly
And joining their tips,
Then turning them, at the crown of the head
Place the fingertips.
In the manner of vajra action,
In the supreme maṇḍala of the whole body,
Display it by the rite of the dance,
And set it firmly at the heart.
Likewise, according to the rite of the dance,
Bind Vajra Protection.
Having bound Vajra Fang,
Press down with the vajra binding.
By the rite of this offering
One becomes able to perform all rites.
Having made the fourfold offering,
Release the mudrā according to the rite.

Their heart-mantras are these:
nṛtya vajra rakṣa vajra khāda vajra banda vajra

Next, one should learn the wisdom of the secret mudrās of the wrathful ones.

With the two hands of the vajra secret,
With a wrathful mind, snap forcefully:
Whatever person's name is spoken,
That one's heart will burst.
Holding the lower lip with the teeth,
Tormenting the one whose name is spoken,
His head too will swiftly split,
The one who has transgressed the command.
With the gaze of the wrathful one, the greatly wrathful,
Screwing up the eyes:
Whoever's name is spoken as one looks,
That one's eyes will burst.
Absorbed in Vajracaṇḍa,
If at one's own heart as well
One binds the vajra binding,
That one's mind will be destroyed.

Here their heart-mantras are these:
hūṃ vajra sphoṭa thaḥ hūṃ vajra muka vajra thaḥ hūṃ vajra netrā thaḥ hūṃ vajra mane thaḥ

Next, one should learn the wisdom of the secret mudrās of the great vajra family. Here, first of all, the binding of the great mudrās is explained.

Binding the little fingers as a hook,
Set up Vajracaṇḍa.
On the left, with the trident behind,
It is called Victorious over the Three Worlds.
The left index finger stretched out straight,
And likewise bent, is the lotus.
And likewise turning it,
Place it on the left vajra.
Binding the vajra at the palm,
Make joy with wrath.
The thumbs with the firm vajra
Are called the wrathful Tirintiri.
The index fingers as a hook are the summoning.
The faces of the index fingers are joined.
Likewise, the two fingers give well.
Joining the fingers, place them at the forehead.
At the heart, the sun stands firm.
With fingers evened, place it on the crown of the head.
Turning it, place it for laughter.
With fingers evened, like a lotus bud.
Both hold the sword straight.
Two fingers bound as a wheel.
With fingers evened, bring them out from the mouth.
With the fingers well extended,
Bind them at the neck with the fingers.
Those two are placed at the cheeks as fangs.
The fist bound tightly
Is said to be the great mudrā.

Next, the binding of the secret samaya mudrās of the vajra family is explained.

Arisen from the secret fist,
They are renowned as the supreme samayas.
Their binding I shall explain:
The vajra binding is unsurpassed.
Placing it at the heart, twist it at the sides of the ribs.
Drawing the arrow from the left,
One should draw it out from the heart.
Turning it into a wrathful frown,
Showing the disk of the sun,
Stretching the hand out at the crown of the head,
And turning it, place it for laughter.
Placed well in the middle of the mouth,
It is like planting a sword at the finger.
Showing the turning of the wheel,
And bringing it forth from the mouth:
At the crown of the head is the maṇḍala of the supreme body.
The shoulders, the heart and the sides of the ribs:
Do Vajra Protection in the same way.
Placing it at the place of the fangs,
And the fist tightly clenched.
The mudrās of the outer maṇḍala
Bind according to the sequence of their signs.
From the samaya vajra binding,
Likewise set the vajra fist.

Next, the secret Dharma mudrās of the vajra family are explained:
phaṭaḥ śaṭaḥ maṭaḥ saṭaḥ raṭaḥ haṭaḥ dhiṭaḥ haṭaḥ phadṭaḥ tadṭaḥ kadṭaḥ dhaḍṭaḥ kuṭiḥ riṭaḥ khaṭaḥ paṭaḥ

Next, the binding of the mudrās of the secret karma family of the vajra family is explained.

In brief, the karma mudrā:
Making two vajra fists,
One should place them at whatever their place is,
With the wrathful gaze, full of wrath.

Here is completed the extensive rite of the maṇḍala of the great mudrās of the secret of the wrathful ones, from the Great King of Ritual Manuals of the Vajra Samaya of All Tathāgatas.

Then again the Blessed One entered the samādhi called Empowerment of the Wisdom Mudrā of the Samādhi of the Vajra Family of All Tathāgatas, and spoke this supreme vidyā:
oṃ sarva tathāgata sūkṣma vajra krodha hūṃ phaṭ

Then Vajrapāṇi, king of the wrathful ones, Victorious over the Three Worlds, spoke this supreme vidyā of the subtle vajra:
oṃ sūṣma vajra krodhe krama hūṃ phaṭ

Then the bodhisattva Vajragarbha spoke this supreme vidyā of the subtle vajra of Victorious over the Three Worlds:
oṃ sūkṣma vajra ratna krodha krama hūṃ phaḍ

Then the bodhisattva Vajranetra spoke this supreme vidyā of the subtle vajra of Victorious over the Three Worlds:
oṃ sūkṣma vajra padma krodha krama hūṃ phaḍ

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajraviśva, spoke this supreme vidyā of the subtle vajra of Victorious over the Three Worlds:
oṃ sūkṣma vajra karma krodha krama hūṃ phaḍ

Then again the Blessed One, Vajrapāṇi, in order to generate his own family, took his place in the manner of the great maṇḍala of Vajradhātu and spoke their heart-mantras:
oṃ vajra satva sūkṣma jñana krodha krama hūṃ phaḍ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra aṃkuśa ākarṣaya mahā krodhā hūṃ phaḍ
oṃ vajra sūkṣma rāga krodhā anurāgayati vikrama hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra sūkṣma vajra puṣṭi krodhā hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra bhṛkuṭī krodhā hara hara hūṃ phaḍ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra jvāla maṇḍala krodhā sūryajvāla hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra cakra krodhā sarva artha māme prayaccha śrī krama hūṃ phaḍ
oṃ vajra sūkṣma hāsa krodhā mahā ṭṭaṭṭa hāsa ha ha ha ha hūṃ phaḍ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra dharma vajra krodhāya hūṃ phaḍ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra chceda krodha cchinda bhinda huṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra krodha mahā cakra cchinda patāya śrī praveśaya hṛdayaṃ bhinda huṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra hūṃ kār hana tāya lvāka matrenna huṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra karma krodha sarva karma kāroṅ bhava sarva kāraya sādhaya huṃ phaṭ
oṃ vajra sūkṣma kavaca krodhā rakṣa rakṣa huṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra yakṣa krodhā hana bhaya sarva duṣṭi ñcitta mātre vajra daṃṣṭrā huṃ phaṭ
oṃ sūkṣma vajra muṣṭi krodha bandha bandha hūṃ phaṭ

Then again Vajrapāṇi spoke the samaya maṇḍala of the subtle wisdom of the vajra family.

Next, what is to be fully explained
Is the sublime Dharma maṇḍala.
Because it resembles the Vajradhātu,
It is also called the Wisdom of the Wrathful Ones.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
Mark out the whole maṇḍala with cords.
In its center draw the Buddha,
Set in the center of a wisdom vajra.
In all directions around the Buddha
Draw all those very buddhas.
Having crossed over with vajra force,
In the four maṇḍalas as well
Victorious over the Three Worlds and the rest
Should be set out in their order.
In all directions around them,
Follow the rite of Vajracaṇḍa.

Then here the extensive sequence of the rite of the subtle wisdom maṇḍala of the vajra family is explained. First of all, having had the disciple enter, the charge is this: "Today the Blessed One, Vajrapāṇi, empowers you as the very vajra wrath of all tathāgatas, so apply yourself to it well and diligently. From acting for the complete protection of the realm of sentient beings, entirely and with none left out, up to the result of attaining the welfare, the happiness and the supreme accomplishment of all tathāgatas, you are to purify even all sentient beings thoroughly by vajra wrath; what need is there even to mention all the vicious ones who act as māras?"

Having given this charge, untie the face covering, show him all their maṇḍalas, and give the vajra into his two hands in the proper way. Then teach him the subtle wisdoms of Vajracaṇḍa.

Through the yoga of Vajra Hūṃ,
Having displayed the subtle vajra,
Whoever "hūṃ" is applied to,
His life will be destroyed.
Having displayed the subtle vajra
And spread it forth according to the rite,
As far as it spreads,
So far is this enemy destroyed.
Through the yoga of Vajra Hūṃ,
Applying the method of the subtle vajra,
